**Pakistan**,[^cite_note-12] officially the **Islamic Republic of Pakistan**,[^cite_note-13] is a country in [South Asia](21566765.md). It is the [fifth-most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population), with a population of over 241.5 million,[^cite_note-fn3-10] having the [second-largest Muslim population in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_by_country#Countries) as of 2023. [Islamabad](51153.md) is the nation's capital, while [Karachi](17123.md) is [its largest city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Pakistan_by_population) and [financial centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre). Pakistan is the [33rd-largest country by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area). Bounded by the [Arabian Sea](2747.md) on the south, the [Gulf of Oman](12896.md) on the southwest, and the [Sir Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Creek) on the southeast, it shares land borders with [India](14533.md) to the east; [Afghanistan](737.md) to the west; [Iran](14653.md) to the southwest; and [China](5405.md) to the northeast. It shares a maritime border with [Oman](22316.md) in the Gulf of Oman, and is separated from [Tajikistan](30108.md) in the northwest by Afghanistan's narrow [Wakhan Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakhan_Corridor).&#x20;

Pakistan is the site of [several ancient cultures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Pakistan), including the 8,500-year-old [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) site of [Mehrgarh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehrgarh) in [Balochistan](4687.md), the [Indus Valley Civilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_Valley_Civilisation) of the [Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze_Age),[^cite_note-Wright-2009-14] and the ancient [Gandhara civilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhara_civilisation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBadian1987-15] The regions that compose the modern state of Pakistan were the realm of multiple empires and dynasties, including the [Achaemenid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaemenid_Empire), the [Maurya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya_Empire), the [Kushan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kushan_Empire), the [Gupta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gupta_Empire);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWynbrandt2009-16] the [Umayyad Caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umayyad_Caliphate) in its southern regions, the [Hindu Shahis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Shahis), the [Ghaznavids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaznavids), the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate), the [Samma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samma_dynasty), the [Shah Miris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Miris), the [Mughals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpuler1969-17] and finally, the [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj) from 1858 to 1947.&#x20;

Spurred by the [Pakistan Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Movement), which sought a homeland for the Muslims of [British India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidencies_and_provinces_of_British_India), and election victories in 1946 by the [All-India Muslim League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-India_Muslim_League), Pakistan gained independence in 1947 after the [partition of British India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_British_India), which awarded separate statehood to its Muslim-majority regions and was accompanied by an unparalleled mass migration and loss of life.[^cite_note-Copland-2001-18][^cite_note-Metcalf-2006-19] Initially [a dominion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_Pakistan) of the [British Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), Pakistan [adopted a republican constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan_\(1956%E2%80%931958\)) in 1956 and became an [Islamic republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_republic) with two geographically separate provinces, [East Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Pakistan) and [West Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Pakistan). East Pakistan seceded as the new country of [Bangladesh](3454.md) in 1971 after a [nine-month-long civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Liberation_War). In the following four decades, Pakistan has been ruled by governments that alternated between civilian and military, democratic and authoritarian, relatively [secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_Pakistan) and Islamist.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETalbot2016-20]&#x20;

Pakistan is considered a [middle power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_power) and is a declared [nuclear-weapon state](400821.md), with the world's [seventh-largest standing armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_military_and_paramilitary_personnel). The [Pakistani economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Pakistan) is ranked amongst the world's emerging and growth-leading economies,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZiaBurton2023-21] with a large and rapidly growing middle class.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERais2017-22][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECornwallEdwards2014-23] Pakistan's political history since independence has been characterised by periods of significant economic and military growth as well as those of political and economic instability. It is an [ethnically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Pakistan) and [linguistically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Pakistan) diverse country, with similarly diverse [geography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Pakistan) and [wildlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Pakistan). The country continues to face [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_Pakistan), [illiteracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literate_Pakistan_Foundation), [corruption](29434819.md), and [terrorism](2816470.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2016-24][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaqir2018-25][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESATP2024-26] Pakistan is a member of the United Nations, the [Shanghai Cooperation Organisation](152422.md), the [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md), the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), the [South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_Association_for_Regional_Cooperation), and the [Islamic Military Counter-Terrorism Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Military_Counter_Terrorism_Coalition), and is designated as a [major non-NATO ally](3812445.md) by the United States.&#x20;

## Etymology

The name *Pakistan* was coined by [Choudhry Rahmat Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choudhry_Rahmat_Ali), a [Pakistan Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Movement) activist, who in January 1933 first published it (originally as "Pakstan") in a pamphlet *[Now or Never](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Declaration)*, using it as an [acronym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acronym).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAziz1987-27][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaqibMalik2018-28][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELahiri2023-29] Rahmat Ali explained: "It is composed of letters taken from the names of all our homelands, Indian and Asian, [**P**anjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab_Province_\(British_India\)), [**A**fghania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North-West_Frontier_Province_\(1901%E2%80%932010\)), [**K**ashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_\(princely_state\)), [**S**indh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sind_Division), and [Baluchis**tan**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baluchistan_Agency)." He added, "Pakistan is both a [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language) and [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) word... It means the land of the Paks, the spiritually pure and clean."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETummala1996-30] Etymologists note that [پاک](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DA%A9#Persian) *pāk*, is 'pure' in Persian and [Pashto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pashto) and the Persian suffix [ـستان](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%D9%80%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%A7%D9%86#Persian) *[-stan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/-stan)* means 'land' or 'place of'.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaqibMalik2018-28]&#x20;

Rahmat Ali's concept of Pakistan only related to the northwestern area of the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent). He also proposed the name "Banglastan" for the Muslim areas of [Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal) and "Osmanistan" for [Hyderabad State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad_State), as well as a political federation between the three.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnand1991-31]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Pakistan)

See also: [Timeline of Pakistani history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Pakistani_history)

### Prehistory and antiquity



*[Priest-King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priest-king_\(sculpture\))* from [Mohenjo-daro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohenjo-daro) (c. 2500 BCE)

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParker2017-32]



Cremation urn, [Gandhara grave culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhara_grave_culture), Swat Valley (c. 1200 BCE)

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurrison2017-33]

Some of the earliest ancient human civilisations in [South Asia](21566765.md) originated from areas encompassing present-day Pakistan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllchinPetraglia2007-34] The earliest known stone tools in the region, dating to the [Lower Palaeolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Palaeolithic) (\~2 million years ago), were discovered in the [Soan Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soan_Valley) of [northern Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Pakistan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERendellDennell1985-35] The [Indus plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_plain) region, which covers most of the present-day Pakistan, was the site of several successive ancient cultures including the [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) (7000–4300 BCE) site of [Mehrgarh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehrgarh),[^cite_note-Coningham-Young-2015-36][^cite_note-Fisher-2018-37][^cite_note-Dyson-2018-38] and the 5,000-year history of urban life in South Asia to the various sites of the [Indus Valley Civilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_Valley_Civilisation), including [Mohenjo-daro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohenjo-daro) and [Harappa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harappa).[^cite_note-Allchin-1982-39][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDalesKenoyerAlcock1986-40]&#x20;

Following the decline of the Indus Valley Civilisation, semi-nomadic [Indo-European Aryans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Aryans) migrated into the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent) around 2000 BCE, perhaps by way of the [Khyber Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khyber_Pass). They fused with the indigenous Harappan culture of the Indus Valley, and elements of the pre-Aryan spiritual traditions were assimilated into the developing Vedic tradition.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEConsiglio2015-41] This cultural milieu shaped the [Gandhara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhara) Civilisation, which flourished at the crossroads of India, Central Asia, and the Middle East, connecting [trade routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road) and absorbing cultural influences from diverse civilisations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBehrendt2007-42] By the early Vedic period, parts of the Indus region in present-day Pakistan were populated by numerous tribes that were beginning to coalesce into chieftain-led clans and early kingdoms.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlood2022-43] During this period, the [Vedas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedas), the oldest [scriptures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scriptures) of [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), were composed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOberlies2023-44][^cite_note-Rigveda-45]&#x20;

### Classical period



*[Standing Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_Buddha)* from [Gandhara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhara) (1st–2nd century CE)

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStonard2017-46]

The western regions of Pakistan [became a part](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaemenid_conquest_of_the_Indus_Valley) of the [Achaemenid Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaemenid_Empire) around 517 BCE.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDandamaev2023-47] In 326 BCE, [Alexander the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_the_Great) conquered the region by defeating various local rulers, most notably, the King [Porus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porus), at [Jhelum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Hydaspes).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESadasivan2011-48] Among the major powers that ruled the region were the [Mauryas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauryas) (322–185 BCE), during which [Ashoka the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka_the_Great) extended the empire.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhan2022114-49][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECooke2017-50] The [Indo-Greek Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Greek_Kingdom) founded by [Demetrius of Bactria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demetrius_I_of_Bactria) (180–165 BCE) included Gandhara and [Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab) and reached its greatest extent under [Menander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menander_I) (165–150 BCE), allowing the [Greco-Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greco-Buddhism) culture in the region to prosper.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPollitt1986-51][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuintanilla2007-52][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubica2023-53] [Taxila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxila) had one of the earliest universities and centres of higher education in the world, which was established during the late Vedic period in the 6th century BCE.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestmoreland2019-54] [The ancient university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_ancient_Taxila) was documented by the invading forces of Alexander the Great and recorded by Chinese pilgrims in the 4th or 5th century CE.[^cite_note-Needham-1994-55][^cite_note-Kulke-Rothermund-2016-56][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMookerji1989-57] At its zenith, the [Rai dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rai_dynasty) (489–632 CE) ruled [Sindh](28972.md) and the surrounding territories.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBanerjee2022-58]&#x20;

### Medieval period

[Makran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makran) came under Muslim rule in the 7th century CE when it was conquered and a garrison was established under [Mu'awiya I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mu%27awiya_I) (r. 661–680).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWink2002-59] The Arab general [Muhammad ibn Qasim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_ibn_Qasim) conquered Sindh and some regions of Punjab in 711 CE.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMufti2013-60] The Pakistan government's official chronology claims this as the time when the foundation of Pakistan was laid.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoodbhoy2023-61] The early medieval period (642–1219 CE) witnessed the spread of Islam in the region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECavendish2006318-62] Before the arrival of Islam beginning in the 8th century, the region of Pakistan was home to a diverse plethora of faiths, including Hinduism, [Buddhism](3267529.md), [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism) and [Zoroastrianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism).[^cite_note-Stubbs-Thomson-2016-63][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMalik200647-64] During this period, [Sufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufi) [missionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawah) played a pivotal role in converting a majority of the regional population to Islam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidus2014-65] Upon the defeat of the [Turk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turk_Shahis) and [Hindu Shahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Shahi) dynasties which governed the [Kabul Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabul_Valley), Gandhara, and western Punjab in the 7th to 11th centuries CE, [several successive Muslim empires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_conquest_in_the_Indian_subcontinent) ruled over the region, including the [Ghaznavid Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaznavid_Empire) (975–1187 CE), the [Ghorid Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghorid_Empire), and the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate) (1206–1526 CE).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESamad2011-66] The [Lodi dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lodi_dynasty), the last of the Delhi Sultanate, was replaced by the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire) (1526–1857 CE).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaroqhi2019-67]&#x20;



[Makli Necropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makli_Necropolis), a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md), rose to prominence as a major funerary site during the [Samma dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samma_dynasty).

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJunejo2020-68]

The Mughals strengthened [Persianate culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persianate_culture) through literature, book production, illustration, and architecture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECanfield2002-69] In the region of modern-day Pakistan, key urban centres during the Mughal period were [Multan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multan), [Lahore](125315.md), [Peshawar](66230.md) and [Thatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thatta),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandra2005-70] which were chosen as the site of impressive [Mughal buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_architecture).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMalik200679-71] In the early 16th century, the region remained under the Mughal Empire.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMetcalfMetcalf2006-72] In the 18th century, the slow disintegration of the Mughal Empire was hastened by the emergence of the rival powers like the [Maratha Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Empire) and later the [Sikh Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikh_Empire), as well as invasions by [Nader Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nader_Shah) from Iran in 1739,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaleem2013-73][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMacDonald2017-74] and the [Durrani Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durrani_Empire) of Afghanistan in 1748.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQassem2016-75] The growing political power of the British in Bengal had not yet reached the territories of modern Pakistan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESimpson2007-76]&#x20;

### Colonial rule

Main articles: [British India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_India), [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj), [Aligarh Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aligarh_Movement), and [Two-nation theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-nation_theory)

Sir Syed Ahmad Khan (1817–1898), whose vision (Two-nation theory) formed the basis of Pakistan

Sir [Syed Ahmad Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Ahmad_Khan) (1817–1898), whose [vision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-nation_theory) formed the basis of Pakistan

[^cite_note-Wolpert-1984-77]

[^cite_note-Sengupta-2023-78]

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoltCurta2016-79]

Muhammad Ali Jinnah (1876–1948) served as Pakistan's first Governor-General and the leader of the Pakistan Movement

[Muhammad Ali Jinnah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_Jinnah) (1876–1948), Pakistan's first Governor-General and the leader of the [Pakistan Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Movement)

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWolpert1984-80]

None of modern Pakistan was under British rule until 1839 when [Karachi](17123.md), a small fishing village governed by [Talpurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talpur_dynasty) of Sindh with a mud fort guarding the harbour, was [taken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Karachi#Company_rule),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERustomji1952-81][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalbridge2012-82] and used as an enclave with a port and [military base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karachi_Cantonment) for the [First Afghan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Afghan_War) that ensued.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGayer2014-83] The remainder of Sindh was acquired in 1843,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESharmaD'AngeloGiri2020-84] and subsequently, through a series of wars and treaties, the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_East_India_Company), and later, after the [Sepoy Mutiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepoy_Mutiny) (1857–1858), direct rule by [Queen Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Victoria) of the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire), acquired most of the region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPirbhai2009-85] Key conflicts included those against the [Baloch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baloch_people) Talpur dynasty, resolved by the [Battle of Miani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Miani) (1843) in Sindh,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarjani2018-86] the [Anglo-Sikh Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Sikh_War_\(disambiguation\)) (1845–1849),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECook1975-87] and the [Anglo-Afghan Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Afghan_Wars) (1839–1919).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhan2022119-88] By 1893, modern Pakistan was part of the [British Indian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Indian_Empire), and remained so until independence in 1947.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECavendish2006365-89]&#x20;

Under British rule, modern Pakistan was primarily divided into the [Sind Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sind_Division), [Punjab Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab_Province_\(British_India\)), and the [Baluchistan Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baluchistan_Agency). The region also included various [princely states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princely_state), with the largest being [Bahawalpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahawalpur_\(princely_state\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELaw1999-90][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHussain2015-91]&#x20;

An important uprising against the British in the region was the [Indian Rebellion of 1857](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rebellion_of_1857), known at the time as the [Sepoy Mutiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepoy_Mutiny).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENyrop1975371-92] Divergence in the [relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu%E2%80%93Islamic_relations) between [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) and Islam resulted in significant tension in [British India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj), leading to religious violence. The [language controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi%E2%80%93Urdu_controversy) further exacerbated tensions between Hindus and Muslims.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoltCurta2016-79][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaliAkhtar1993-93] A [Muslim intellectual movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aligarh_Movement), led by Sir [Syed Ahmed Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Ahmed_Khan) to counter the [Hindu renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_Renaissance), advocated for the [two-nation theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-nation_theory) and led to the establishment of the [All-India Muslim League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-India_Muslim_League) in 1906.[^cite_note-Wolpert-1984-77][^cite_note-Sengupta-2023-78][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoltCurta2016-79]&#x20;

In March 1929, in response to the [Nehru Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nehru_Report), [Muhammad Ali Jinnah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_Jinnah), the founder of Pakistan, issued his [fourteen points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourteen_Points_of_Jinnah), which included proposals to safeguard the interests of the Muslim minority in a united India. These proposals were rejected.[^cite_note-Hardy-1972-94][^cite_note-Wuthnow-2013-95][^cite_note-Singh-Shani-2021-96] In his 29 December 1930 address, [Allama Iqbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Iqbal) advocated the amalgamation of Muslim-majority states in [North-West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_India_\(pre-1947\)) India, including [Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab_Province_\(British_India\)), [North-West Frontier Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North-West_Frontier_Province_\(1901%E2%80%9355\)), [Sind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sind_Province_\(1936%E2%80%9355\)), and [Baluchistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baluchistan_\(Chief_Commissioner%27s_Province\)).[^cite_note-Singh-Shani-2021-96][^cite_note-Iqbal-97] The perception that Congress-led British [provincial governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1937_Indian_provincial_elections) neglected the Muslim League from 1937 to 1939 motivated Jinnah and other Muslim League leaders to embrace the two-nation theory.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPandeya2003-98][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBasuMiroshnik2017-99] This led to the adoption of the [Lahore Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lahore_Resolution) of 1940, presented by [Sher-e-Bangla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sher-e-Bangla) [A.K. Fazlul Haque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.K._Fazlul_Haque), also known as the Pakistan Resolution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEM._H._Khan2016-100]&#x20;

By 1942, Britain faced considerable strain during [World War II](32927.md), with India directly threatened by Japanese forces. Britain had pledged voluntary independence for India in exchange for support during the war. However, this pledge included a clause stating that no part of British India would be compelled to join the resulting dominion, which could be interpreted as support for an independent Muslim nation. Congress under the leadership of [Mahatma Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi) launched the [Quit India Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quit_India_Movement), demanding an immediate end to British rule. In contrast, the Muslim League chose to support the [UK's war efforts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_in_World_War_II), thereby nurturing the possibility of establishing a Muslim nation.[^cite_note-Tucker-2020-101][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandra2008-102]&#x20;

### Independence

Main article: [Pakistan Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Movement)

Further information: [Indian independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_independence_movement) and [Partition of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_India)



The [partition of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_India): green regions were all part of Pakistan by 1948, and orange ones part of India. The darker-shaded regions represent the [Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab_Province_\(British_India\)) and [Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Presidency) provinces partitioned by the [Radcliffe Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radcliffe_Line). The grey areas represent some of the key [princely states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princely_state) that were eventually integrated into India or Pakistan.

The [1946 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Indian_provincial_elections) saw the Muslim League secure 90 percent of the Muslim seats, supported by the landowners of Sindh and Punjab. This forced the Indian National Congress, initially skeptical of the League's representation of Indian Muslims, to acknowledge its significance.[^cite_note-Mohiuddin-2007-1-103] Jinnah's emergence as the voice of the Indian Muslims,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoodbhoy2023-61] compelled the British to consider their stance, despite their [reluctance to partition India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_to_the_partition_of_India). In a final attempt to prevent partition, they proposed the [Cabinet Mission Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_Mission_Plan).[^cite_note-Mohiuddin-2007-2-104]&#x20;

As the Cabinet Mission failed, the British announced their intention to end rule by June 1948.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWolpert1984309-105][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarkovits2012-106] Following rigorous discussions involving [Viceroy of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_India), [Lord Mountbatten of Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Mountbatten,_1st_Earl_Mountbatten_of_Burma), [Muhammad Ali Jinnah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_Jinnah) of the [All-India Muslim League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-India_Muslim_League), and [Jawaharlal Nehru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru) of Congress, the formal declaration to partition British India into two independent dominions—namely Pakistan and India—was issued by Mountbatten on the evening of 3 June 1947. In Mountbatten's oval office, the prime ministers of around a dozen major princely states gathered to receive their copies of the plan before its worldwide broadcast. At 7\:00 P.M., [All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio) transmitted the public announcement, starting with the viceroy's address, followed by individual speeches from Nehru, and Jinnah. The founder of Pakistan [Muhammad Ali Jinnah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_Jinnah) concluded his address with the slogan *Pakistan Zindabad* (Long Live Pakistan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWolpert1984328–329-107]&#x20;

As the United Kingdom agreed to the [partitioning of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_India),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWolpert1984328–329-107] the modern state of Pakistan was established on [14 August 1947](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(Pakistan\)) (27th of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan) in 1366 of the [Islamic calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_calendar), [a holy time regarded as auspicious and noted for its religious importance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_of_Power)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHakeem2014-108][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENDTV2025-109] This new nation amalgamated the [Muslim-majority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_India) eastern and [northwestern regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_India_\(pre-1947\)) of [British India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_India), comprising the provinces of [Balochistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balochistan_\(Pakistan\)), [East Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Bengal), the [North-West Frontier Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North-West_Frontier_Province_\(1901%E2%80%931955\)), [West Punjab](24751.md), and Sindh.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen20046-110]&#x20;

In the riots that accompanied the partition in Punjab Province, between 200,000 and 2,000,000 people were killed in what some have described as a retributive genocide between the religions.[^cite_note-Riots-1-111] Around 50,000 Muslim women were [abducted and raped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violence_against_women_during_the_partition_of_India) by Hindu and Sikh men, while 33,000 Hindu and Sikh women experienced the same fate at the hands of Muslims.[^cite_note-Riots-2-112] Around 6.5 million Muslims moved from India to West Pakistan and 4.7 million Hindus and Sikhs moved from West Pakistan to India.[^cite_note-Hasan-Raza-2009-113] It was the largest mass migration in human history.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERiggs2024-114] A subsequent dispute over the [princely state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princely_state) of [Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_\(princely_state\)) eventually sparked the [Indo-Pakistani War of 1947–1948](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_War_of_1947%E2%80%931948).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBhaumik1996-115]&#x20;

### Post-independence

Main articles: [History of Pakistan (1947–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Pakistan_\(1947%E2%80%93present\)) and [Dominion of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_Pakistan)



[Liaquat Ali Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liaquat_Ali_Khan) was elected the first prime minister of Pakistan.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKazmi2003-116]

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETucker2017-117]

After [independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creation_of_Pakistan) in 1947, Jinnah, the [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_chair) of the Muslim League, became Pakistan's first [governor-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_Pakistan) and the first [President-Speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_National_Assembly_of_Pakistan) of the [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Pakistan), but he died of tuberculosis on 11 September 1948.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETucker2017-117][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAkbar2018-118] Meanwhile, Pakistan's founding fathers agreed to appoint [Liaquat Ali Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liaquat_Ali_Khan), the [secretary-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_General) of the [party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-India_Muslim_League), the nation's [first prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_Pakistan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKazmi2003-116][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETucker2017-117] From 1947 to 1956, [Pakistan was a monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Pakistan) within the Commonwealth of Nations, and had two monarchs before it became a republic.[^cite_note-Kumarasingham-2013-119]&#x20;

The creation of Pakistan was never fully accepted by many British leaders, including [Lord Mountbatten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Mountbatten,_1st_Earl_Mountbatten_of_Burma).[^cite_note-McGrath-1996-120] Mountbatten expressed his lack of support and faith in the Muslim League's idea of Pakistan.[^cite_note-Ahmed-1997-121] Jinnah refused Mountbatten's offer to serve as Governor-General of Pakistan.[^cite_note-Wolpert-2009-122] When Mountbatten was asked by [Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Collins_\(writer\)) and [Lapierre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominique_Lapierre) if he would have sabotaged Pakistan had he known that Jinnah was dying of tuberculosis, he replied 'most probably'.[^cite_note-Lapierre-Collins-2015-123]&#x20;

The American [CIA](5183633.md) film on Pakistan, made in 1950, examines the history and geography of Pakistan.

> "You are free; you are free to go to your temples, you are free to go to your mosques or to any other place of worship in this State of Pakistan. You may belong to any religion or caste or creed – that has nothing to do with the business of the State."&#x20;

— [Muhammad Ali Jinnah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_Jinnah)'s first speech to the Constituent Assembly of Pakistan.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilson2009-124]

Maulana [Shabbir Ahmad Usmani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabbir_Ahmad_Usmani), a respected Deobandi *alim* (scholar) who held the position of Shaykh al-Islam in Pakistan in 1949, and [Maulana Mawdudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abul_A%27la_Maududi) of [Jamaat-i-Islami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaat-e-Islami) played key roles in advocating for an Islamic constitution. Mawdudi insisted that the Constituent Assembly declare the "supreme sovereignty of God" and the supremacy of the *shariah* in Pakistan.[^cite_note-Hussain-2008-1-125]&#x20;

The efforts of Jamaat-i-Islami and the *[ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulama)* led to the passage of the [Objectives Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Objectives_Resolution) in March 1949. This resolution, described by Liaquat Ali Khan as the second most significant step in Pakistan's history, affirmed that "sovereignty over the entire universe belongs to God Almighty alone and the authority which He has delegated to the State of Pakistan through its people for being exercised within the limits prescribed by Him is a sacred trust". It was later included as a preamble to the constitutions of 1956, 1962, and 1973.[^cite_note-Hussain-2008-2-126]&#x20;

[Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_in_Pakistan) faced setbacks due to the [martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_Pakistani_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat) imposed by President [Iskander Mirza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iskander_Mirza), who was succeeded by General [Ayub Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayub_Khan_\(President_of_Pakistan\)). After adopting a [presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan_of_1962) in 1962, Pakistan witnessed significant growth until the [second war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_War_of_1965) with India in 1965, resulting in an economic downturn and widespread public discontent in 1967.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWynbrandt2009190–197-127][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChowdhuryMahmud2008-128] In 1969, President [Yahya Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Khan) consolidated control, but faced a devastating [cyclone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_Bhola_cyclone) in East Pakistan resulting in 500,000 deaths.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKathpalia1986-129]&#x20;

In 1970, Pakistan conducted its [first democratic elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_Pakistani_general_election) since independence, intending to transition from [military rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_Framework_Order,_1970) to democracy. However, after the East Pakistani [Awami League](404861.md) emerged victorious over the [Pakistan Peoples Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Peoples_Party) (PPP), Yahya Khan and the military refused to transfer power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoumar2023-130] This led to [Operation Searchlight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Searchlight), a military crackdown, and eventually sparked the [war of liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Liberation_War) by Bengali [Mukti Bahini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukti_Bahini) forces in East Pakistan,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis2011-131] described in West Pakistan as a civil war rather than a liberation struggle.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBose2005-132]&#x20;



Signing of the [Tashkent Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tashkent_Declaration) to end [hostilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_War_of_1965) with India in 1965 in [Tashkent](57533.md), [USSR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics_\(USSR\)), by President [Ayub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_Marshal_Ayub_Khan) alongside [Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zulfikar_Ali_Bhutto) (centre) and [Aziz Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aziz_Ahmed_\(civil_servant\)) (left)

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhan2008-133]

Independent researchers estimate that between 300,000 and 500,000 civilians died during this period while the Bangladesh government puts the number of dead at three million,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESunkaraWalterRojas2024-134] a figure now nearly universally regarded as excessively inflated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHiro2015-135] Some academics such as [Rudolph Rummel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudolph_Rummel) and [Rounaq Jahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rounaq_Jahan) say both sides committed genocide;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERummel1998-136] others such as [Richard Sisson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Richard_Sisson) and Leo E. Rose believe there was no genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBeachler2011-137] In response to India's support for the insurgency in East Pakistan, [preemptive strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preemptive_strikes) on India by Pakistan's [air force](377433.md), [navy](1183789.md), and [marines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Marines) sparked [a conventional war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_War_of_1971) in 1971 that resulted in an Indian victory and East Pakistan gaining [independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Bangladesh) as [Bangladesh](3454.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETotten2000-138]&#x20;

Yahya Khan was replaced by [Zulfikar Ali Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zulfikar_Ali_Bhutto) as president; the country worked towards promulgating [its constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan) and putting the country on the road to democracy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPaxton2016-139][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOldenburg2010-140] In 1972 Pakistan embarked on an ambitious plan to develop its [nuclear deterrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_and_its_Nuclear_Deterrent_Program) capability with [the goal of preventing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutually_assured_destruction) any [foreign invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_interventionism); the country's [first](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KANUPP) [nuclear power plant](207347.md) was inaugurated in that same year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFitzpatrick2007-141][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoodbhoy2011-142] India's [first nuclear test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Smiling_Buddha) in 1974 gave Pakistan additional justification to accelerate its [nuclear program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_and_Nuclear_Weapons).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoodbhoy2011-142]&#x20;

Democracy ended with a [military coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Fair_Play) in 1977 against the [leftist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_in_Pakistan) PPP, which saw [General Zia-ul-Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Zia-ul-Haq) become the president in 1978.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKrasnoLaPides2015-143] From 1977 to 1988, President Zia's [corporatisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_sector_of_Pakistan) and [economic Islamisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamization_of_Economy) initiatives led to Pakistan becoming one of the fastest-growing economies in South Asia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhanna2002-144] While building up the country's [nuclear program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_and_its_Nuclear_Deterrent_Program), increasing [Islamisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zia-ul-Haq%27s_Islamization), and the rise of a homegrown [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Pakistan) philosophy, Pakistan helped subsidise and distribute US [resources to factions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cyclone) of the [mujahideen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_mujahideen) against [the USSR's intervention in communist Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet%E2%80%93Afghan_War).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHajari2015-145][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEColl2004-146][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestad2005-147] Pakistan's [North-West Frontier Province](21950.md) became a base for the anti-Soviet Afghan fighters, with the province's influential [Deobandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deobandi) ulama playing a significant role in encouraging and organising the 'jihad'.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaroon2008-148]&#x20;

[President Zia died](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Muhammad_Zia-ul-Haq) in a plane crash in 1988, and [Benazir Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto), daughter of Zulfikar Ali Bhutto, was elected as the country's [first female](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_Muslim_societies) Prime Minister. The PPP was followed by conservative [Pakistan Muslim League (N)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Muslim_League_\(N\)) (PML (N)), and over the next decade the leaders of the two parties fought for power, alternating in office.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETucker2015-149] This period is marked by prolonged [stagflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periods_of_stagflation_in_Pakistan), political instability, [corruption](29434819.md), misgovernment, [geopolitical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geostrategy) rivalry with India, and the clash between [left-wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_in_Pakistan) and [right-wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Pakistan) ideologies.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChapman2018-150][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHusain2010-151] As PML (N) secured a [supermajority](638313.md) in [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Pakistani_general_election) in 1997,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYapAbeyratne202368-152] [Nawaz Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawaz_Sharif) authorised [nuclear testings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_nuclear_weapons_tests_of_Pakistan), as a [retaliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tit-for-tat) to the [second nuclear tests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pokhran-II) conducted by India in May 1998.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhan2012-153]&#x20;



President [Musharraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf) meets with Indian Prime Minister [Vajpayee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee) in [Islamabad](51153.md) at the sidelines of the 12th [SAARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAARC) summit in 2004.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAhmad2023-154]

Military tension between the two countries in the [Kargil district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kargil_district) led to the [Kargil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kargil_War) of 1999,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMazari2003-155][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChakma2014-156] and turmoil in [civil-military relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil-military_relations) allowed General [Pervez Musharraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf) to take over through a [bloodless coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Pakistani_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYarbakhsh2019-157] Musharraf governed Pakistan as [chief executive](37570.md) from 1999 to 2002 and as president from 2001 to 2008[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhoja-Moolji2021-158]—a period of [enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enlightened_moderation),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFair2014-159][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKennedy2021-160] social [liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_islam),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbid2023-161] extensive [economic reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_reforms_in_Pakistan),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMohiuddin2007219-162] and [direct involvement in the US-led war on terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan%27s_role_in_the_War_on_Terror).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKennedy2021-160] By its own financial calculations, Pakistan's involvement in the war on terrorism has cost up to \$118 billion, over [eighty one thousand casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_terrorist_incidents_in_Pakistan_since_2001),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMartiniFordJackson2020-163] and more than 1.8 million displaced civilians.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMansbachPirroTaylor2017-164]&#x20;

The [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_\(Pakistan\)) historically completed its first full five-year term on 15 November 2007.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_States_Senate_Committee_on_Foreign_Relations2008-165] After the [assassination of Benazir Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Benazir_Bhutto) in 2007, the PPP secured the [most votes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority) in the [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Pakistani_general_election) of 2008, appointing party member [Yusuf Raza Gilani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yusuf_Raza_Gilani) as Prime Minister.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJaffrelot2015261-166] Threatened with [impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movement_to_impeach_Pervez_Musharraf), President Musharraf resigned on 18 August 2008, and was succeeded by [Asif Ali Zardari](993655.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKapoor2009-167] Clashes with the [judicature](4988685.md) prompted Gilani's disqualification from the [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Pakistan) and as the prime minister in June 2012.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaseem2022-168] The [general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Pakistani_general_election) held in 2013 saw the PML (N) achieve victory,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDedeSadioglu2016-169] following which Nawaz Sharif was elected as Prime Minister for the third time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERuhland2019-170] In 2018, [PTI](1651925.md) won the [general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Pakistan_general_election) and [Imran Khan](413184.md) became the 22nd Prime Minister.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurnett2020-171] In April 2022, [Shehbaz Sharif](6502561.md) was elected as prime minister, after Imran Khan lost a no-confidence vote.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2023-172] During [2024 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Pakistani_general_election), PTI-backed independents became the largest bloc,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAfzal2024-173] but Shehbaz Sharif was elected prime minister for a second term, as a result of a coalition between PML (N) and PPPP.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETariqStenson2024-174]&#x20;

## Geography

Main articles: [Geography of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Pakistan), [Environment of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_Pakistan), [Climate of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Pakistan), [Extreme weather records in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_extreme_weather_records_in_Pakistan), [List of tropical cyclones in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tropical_cyclones_in_Pakistan), and [List of beaches in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_beaches_in_Pakistan)



[Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) of Pakistan

Pakistan's diverse [geography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Pakistan) and [climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Pakistan) host a wide array of [wildlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Pakistan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECheng_et_al.2022-175] Covering 881,913 km<sup>2</sup> (340,509 sq mi),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgarwalAhmad2021-176] Pakistan ranks as the [33rd-largest nation by total area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_area),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMordiAdisa2022-177] but this varies based on Kashmir's disputed status. Pakistan boasts a 1,046 km (650 mi) coastline along the Arabian Sea and the Gulf of Oman,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaque2002-178][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBritannica_(Gulf_of_Oman)2024-179] and shares land borders totalling 6,774 km (4,209 mi), including 2,430 km (1,510 mi) [with Afghanistan](203570.md), 523 km (325 mi) [with China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Pakistan_border), 2,912 km (1,809 mi) [with India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Pakistan_border), and 909 km (565 mi) [with Iran](11715031.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDawn2009a-180] It has a [maritime border with Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oman%E2%80%93Pakistan_maritime_boundary)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKaraman2012-181] and is separated from Tajikistan via the narrow strip of the [Wakhan Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakhan_Corridor).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBanerjee2019-182] Situated at the crossroads of South Asia, the Middle East, and Central Asia,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMohiuddin20073,_317,_323–324-183] Pakistan's location is geopolitically significant.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKreft2007-184] Geologically, Pakistan lies at the interaction of the [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Plate) and [Eurasian tectonic plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Plate) within the Indus–Tsangpo Suture Zone, a region of high seismic activity. The collision between these two plates occurs in [northwest Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Pakistan), particularly in [Khyber Pakhtunkhwa](21950.md). Sindh and Punjab are located on the north-western corner of the Indian Plate, while Balochistan and much of Khyber Pakhtunkhwa lie along its north-western margin. [Northern areas of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Pakistan), including Azad Kashmir, lie along the northern edge of the Indian Plate and are [prone to powerful earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_earthquakes_in_Pakistan).[^cite_note-Geology-185]&#x20;



A satellite image showing the topography of Pakistan

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDescloitres2002-186]

Pakistan's landscapes vary from coastal plains to glaciated mountains, offering deserts, forests, hills, and plateaus.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECavendish2006297-187] Pakistan is divided into three major geographic areas: the northern highlands, the Indus River plain, and the Balochistan Plateau.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlood199682-188] The northern highlands feature the [Karakoram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karakoram), [Hindu Kush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Kush), and [Pamir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamir_Mountains) mountain ranges, hosting some of the world's highest peaks, including five of the fourteen [eight-thousanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eight-thousanders) (mountain peaks over 8,000 metres or 26,250 feet), notably [K2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K2) (8,611 m or 28,251 ft) and [Nanga Parbat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanga_Parbat) (8,126 m or 26,660 ft).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiwani2021-189][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBright2017-190] The Balochistan Plateau lies in the west and the [Thar Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thar_Desert) in the east.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlood199683-191][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAhmad2009-192][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHasanRaza200910-193] The 1,609 km (1,000 mi) Indus River and its tributaries traverse the nation from Kashmir to the Arabian Sea, sustaining alluvial plains along the Punjab and Sindh regions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESamuel2016-194]&#x20;

The climate varies from tropical to temperate, with arid conditions in the coastal south. There is a monsoon season with frequent flooding due to heavy rainfall, and a dry season with significantly less rainfall or none at all.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandrappaGuptaKulshrestha2011-195] Pakistan experiences four distinct seasons: a cool, dry winter from December through February; a hot, dry spring from March through May; the summer rainy season, or southwest monsoon period, from June through September; and the retreating monsoon period of October and November.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlood199687-196] Rainfall varies greatly from year to year, with patterns of alternate flooding and drought common.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELaneNortonRyan2017-197]&#x20;

### Flora and fauna

Main articles: [Wildlife of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Pakistan), [Flora of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_Pakistan), and [Fauna of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna_of_Pakistan)

The diverse landscape and climate in Pakistan support a wide range of trees and plants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEl-Esawi2019-198] From coniferous [alpine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_plant) and [subalpine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subalpine) trees like [spruce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spruce), [pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pine), and [deodar cedar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deodar_cedar) in the northern mountains to [deciduous trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deciduous_trees) like [shisham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shisham) in the [Sulaiman Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulaiman_Mountains),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandrappaGuptaKulshrestha2011-195] and palms such as [coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut) and [date](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenix_dactylifera) in the southern regions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbul-Soad2011-199][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDescals_et_al.2023-200] The western hills boast [juniper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juniper), [tamarisk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamarisk), coarse grasses, and scrub plants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpateLearmonth2017-201] [Mangrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) forests dominate the coastal wetlands in the south.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESandhu2010-202] Coniferous forests span altitudes from 1,000 to 4,000 metres (3,300 to 13,100 feet) in most northern and northwestern highlands.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNEP-WCMC2024-203] In Balochistan's xeric regions, date palms and *[Ephedra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ephedra_\(genus\))* are prevalent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbul-Soad2011-199][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAkhtarMirza2006-204] In Punjab and Sindh's Indus plains, tropical and subtropical dry and moist broadleaf forests as well as tropical and xeric shrublands thrive.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPEPA2016-205] Approximately 4.8% or 36,845.6 square kilometres (3,684,560 ha) of Pakistan was forested in 2021.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank2024-206][^cite_note-207]&#x20;



[Markhor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markhor) is the national animal of Pakistan.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFatima2020-208]

Pakistan's fauna mirrors its diverse climate. The country boasts around 668 bird species,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaridah-HanumHakeemÖztürkEfe2015-209] including [crows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crow), [sparrows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_World_sparrow), [mynas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acridotheres), [hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawk), [falcons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falcon), and [eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle). [Palas, Kohistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palas,_Kohistan), is home to the [western tragopan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_tragopan), with many migratory birds visiting from Europe, Central Asia, and India.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrimmettInskipp2021-210] The southern plains harbor [mongooses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongoose),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHunter2018-211] [small Indian civet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Indian_civet),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESanBelantSatoSomers2021-212] hares,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFluxChapman1990-213] the [Asiatic jackal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asiatic_jackal),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESrinivasuluSrinivasulu2012-214] the [Indian pangolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_pangolin),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaseem_et_al.2020-215] the [jungle cat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jungle_cat),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESunquistSunquist2014-216] and the [sand cat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sand_cat).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESunquistSunquist2017-217] Indus is home to [mugger crocodiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugger_crocodile),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman2021-218] while surrounding areas host [wild boars](52255.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETisdell2013-219] deer,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESrinivasulu2018-220] and [porcupines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_porcupine).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERoze2012-221] Central Pakistan's sandy scrublands shelter Asiatic jackals,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESrinivasuluSrinivasulu2012-214] [striped hyenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Striped_hyena),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESomerville2021-222] wildcats, and [leopards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard). The mountainous north hosts a variety of animals like the [Marco Polo sheep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Polo_sheep),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENyrop197574-223] [urial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urial), [markhor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markhor) goat, [ibex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibex) goat, [Asian black bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_black_bear), and [Himalayan brown bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himalayan_brown_bear).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandrappaGuptaKulshrestha2011-195]&#x20;

The lack of vegetative cover, severe climate, and grazing impact on deserts have endangered wild animals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECBD_Report2009-224] The [chinkara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinkara) is the only animal found in significant numbers in [Cholistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholistan),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMallonKingswood2001-225] with a few [nilgai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgai) along the Pakistan–India border and in some parts of Cholistan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodsMuftiHasan1997-226] Rare animals include the [snow leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snow_leopard) and the blind [Indus river dolphin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_river_dolphin),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandrappaGuptaKulshrestha2011-195] of which there are believed to be about 1,816 remaining, protected at the [Indus Dolphin Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_Dolphin_Reserve) in Sindh.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWWF2024-227] In total, 174 species of mammals, 177 species of reptiles, 22 species of amphibians, 198 species of freshwater fish, 668 species of birds, over 5,000 species of insects, and over 5,700 species of plants have been recorded in Pakistan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaridah-HanumHakeemÖztürkEfe2015-209] Pakistan faces deforestation, hunting, and pollution, with a 2019 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index) mean score of 7.42/10, ranking 41st globally out of 172 countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrantham_et_al.2020-228]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Government of Pakistan](1897833.md), [Politics of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Pakistan), and [Elections in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Pakistan)



[Parliament House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Pakistan)

Pakistan operates as a democratic [parliamentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic) [federal republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_republic), with Islam designated as the [state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInter-Parliamentary_Union1973-229][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMunir1975-230] The military's influence in mainstream politics has been significant throughout Pakistan's history.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2004-231] Initially adopting a [constitution in 1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan_of_1956), Pakistan saw it suspended by Ayub Khan in 1958, replaced by a [second constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan_of_1962) in 1962.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen200465-232] Under the leadership of [Zulfikar Ali Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zulfikar_Ali_Bhutto), the [1973 constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan) established a parliamentary system, but it was suspended under [Zia-ul-Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zia-ul-Haq)'s martial law in 1977 and restored, with amendments, in 1985.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeVotta2015-233] The eras of 1958–1971, [1977–1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977_Pakistani_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat), and 1999–2008 witnessed [military coups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_coups_in_Pakistan), leading to [martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law_in_Pakistan) and military leaders governing de facto as presidents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETertraisSokolski2013-234] Presently, Pakistan operates a [multi-party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-party_system) [parliamentary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeBreenAllison-Reumann2023-235] with distinct [checks and balances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Check_and_balance) among government branches.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBloor2023-236] The first successful [democratic transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Pakistani_general_election) occurred in May 2013.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB._Chakma2014-237] Pakistani politics revolves around a blend of [socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_in_Pakistan), [conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Pakistan), and the [third way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_way),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChengappa2002-238] with the three main political parties being the conservative [Pakistan Muslim League (N)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_Muslim_League_\(N\)), socialist [Pakistani People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistani_People%27s_Party), and centrist [Pakistan Tehreek-e-Insaf](1651925.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECRS2023-239] Constitutional amendments in 2010 curtailed presidential powers, enhancing the role of the prime minister.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERafiqAhmad2016-240]&#x20;

- [Head of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_State): The ceremonial head of the state and civilian [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the [Pakistan Armed Forces](23369.md) is the [President](419460.md), elected by an [Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_College_of_Pakistan).

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTECRS2023-239]

  &#x20;The [prime minister](461896.md) advises the president on key appointments, including military and judicial positions, and the president is constitutionally bound to act on this advice.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAziz2018-241]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEF._Hussain2015-242]

  &#x20;The president also holds powers to pardon and grant clemency.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMahmood1965-243]

- [Legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative): The [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral) legislature includes a 96-member [Senate](761025.md) ([upper house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_House)) and a 336-member [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Pakistan) ([lower house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_House)). [National Assembly members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_National_Assembly_of_Pakistan) are elected via [first-past-the-post](261709.md) under [universal adult suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_adult_suffrage), representing National Assembly [constituencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constituencies_of_Pakistan). The constitution reserves 70 seats for women and religious minorities, allocated to political parties based on proportional representation. Senate members are elected by provincial legislators, ensuring equal representation across all provinces.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYapAbeyratne2023272-244]



[Prime Minister's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister%27s_Office_\(Pakistan\))

- Executive: The prime minister, typically the leader of the [majority rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_rule) party or coalition in the National Assembly (the lower house),

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDowdingDumont2014-245]

  &#x20;serves as the country's chief executive and head of government. Responsibilities include forming a [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Pakistan),

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZierkeStockmannMeyer2023-246]

  &#x20;making executive decisions,

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAziz2018-241]

  &#x20;and appointing senior civil servants, subject to executive confirmation.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEstablishment_Division2013-247]

- [Provincial governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_provincial_governments_of_Pakistan): Each of the [four provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Provinces_\(Pakistan\)) follows a similar governance system, with a directly elected [Provincial Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Provincial_Assemblies_of_Pakistan) choosing the [Chief Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_\(Pakistan\)), usually from the largest party or coalition. Chief Ministers lead the provincial cabinet and oversee provincial governance.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMahmood2007-248]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIFES2013-249]

  &#x20;The [Chief Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Secretary_\(Pakistan\)), appointed by the prime minister, heads the provincial bureaucracy.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEstablishment_Division2021-250]

  &#x20;Provincial assemblies legislate and approve the provincial budget, typically presented by the provincial finance minister annually.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIFES2013-249]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAhmadAsif2007-251]

  &#x20;Ceremonial heads of provinces, the Provincial Governors, are appointed by the president based on the binding advice of the prime minister.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMahmood2007-248]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTESenate_of_Pakistan2018-252]



[Supreme Court of Pakistan](843857.md)

- [Judicature](4988685.md): The judiciary in Pakistan has two classes: the superior and subordinate judiciary. The superior judiciary includes the [Supreme Court of Pakistan](843857.md),

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWuBandyopadhyayLee2021-253]

  &#x20;[Federal Shariat Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Shariat_Court), and five [high courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_courts_of_Pakistan),

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJha2016-254]

  &#x20;with the Supreme Court at the top. It's responsible for safeguarding the constitution.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWuBandyopadhyayLee2021-253]

  &#x20;Azad Kashmir and Gilgit-Baltistan have their own court systems.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOberst2018-255]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEjaz2022-256]

### Role of Islam

See also: [Islam in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Pakistan)

Pakistan, the only country established in the name of Islam,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEsposito2003-257] had overwhelming support among Muslims, especially in provinces like the [United Provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Provinces_\(1937%E2%80%9350\)), where Muslims were a minority.[^cite_note-Dhulipala-2015-1-258] This idea, articulated by the Muslim League, the [Islamic clergy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulama), and Jinnah, envisioned an [Islamic state](2646438.md).[^cite_note-Dhulipala-2015-2-259] Jinnah, closely associated with the *ulama*, was described upon his death by [Maulana Shabbir Ahmad Usmani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maulana_Shabbir_Ahmad_Usmani) as the greatest Muslim after [Aurangzeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangzeb), aspiring to unite Muslims worldwide under Islam.[^cite_note-Dhulipala-2015-3-260]&#x20;

The Objectives Resolution of March 1949 marked the initial step towards this goal, affirming God as the sole sovereign.[^cite_note-Hussain-2008-2-126][^cite_note-Haqqani-2010-1-261] Muslim League leader [Chaudhry Khaliquzzaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaudhry_Khaliquzzaman) asserted that Pakistan could only truly become an Islamic state after bringing all believers of Islam into a single political unit.[^cite_note-Dhulipala-2015-4-262] Keith Callard observed that Pakistanis believed in the essential unity of purpose and outlook in the Muslim world, expecting similar views on religion and nationality from Muslims worldwide.[^cite_note-Haqqani-2010-2-263]&#x20;



[Eid prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eid_prayers) at the [Badshahi Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badshahi_Mosque) in Lahore

Pakistan's desire for a united Islamic bloc, called Islamistan, wasn't supported by other Muslim governments,[^cite_note-Haqqani-2010-3-264] though figures like the Grand Mufti of Palestine, [Al-Haj Amin al-Husseini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amin_al-Husseini), and leaders of the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md) were drawn to the country. Pakistan's desire for an international organisation of Muslim countries was fulfilled in the 1970s when the [Organisation of Islamic Conference](47725.md) (OIC) was formed.[^cite_note-Haqqani-2010-4-265] In East Pakistan, Bengali Muslims increasingly prioritised Bengali ethno-nationalism over religious nationalism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENazrul_IslamSaidul_Islam2018-266] The Islamist party [Jamaat-e-Islami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaat-e-Islami) backed an Islamic state and opposed [Bengali nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_nationalism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELintner2002-267]&#x20;

After the 1970 general elections, the Parliament crafted the [1973 Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan).[^cite_note-Diamantides-Gearey-2011-1-268] It declared Pakistan an Islamic Republic, with Islam as the state religion, and mandated laws to comply with Islamic teachings laid down in the [Quran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran) and [Sunnah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunnah) and that no law repugnant to such injunctions could be enacted.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIqbal2009-269] Additionally, it established institutions like the Shariat Court and the [Council of Islamic Ideology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Islamic_Ideology) to interpret and apply Islam.[^cite_note-Diamantides-Gearey-2011-2-270]&#x20;

Zulfikar Ali Bhutto faced opposition under the banner of *Nizam-e-Mustafa* ("Rule of the [Prophet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad)"),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENasr1996-271] advocating an Islamic state. Bhutto conceded to some Islamist demands before being ousted in a coup.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKepel2006-272]&#x20;

General Zia-ul-Haq, after seizing power, committed to establishing an Islamic state and enforcing *[sharia](28840.md)* law.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKepel2006-272] He instituted Shariat judicial courts,[^cite_note-Diamantides-Gearey-2011-3-273] and court benches,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaqqani2010400-274][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsia_Watch_Committee_(U.S.)1992-275] to adjudicate using Islamic doctrine.[^cite_note-Wynbrandt-2009-276] Zia aligned with Deobandi institutions,[^cite_note-Syed-Pio-Kamran-Zaidi-2016-1-277] exacerbating sectarian tensions with anti-Shia policies.[^cite_note-Syed-Pio-Kamran-Zaidi-2016-2-278]&#x20;

Most Pakistanis, according to a [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) (PEW) poll, favour Sharia law as the official law,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZiegfeld2016-279] and 94 percent of them identify more with religion than nationality compared to Muslims in other nations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPRC2011-280]&#x20;

### Administrative units

Main article: [Administrative units of Pakistan](446017.md)

| Administrative unit[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENee2013-281] | Capital[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWasti2009-282][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchuurmans202363-283][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFischer-TahirNaumann2013-284] | Population[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPBS2023-285][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHussain2020-286][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis2023-287] |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Balochistan](4687.md) | [Quetta](93764.md) | 14,894,402 |
| [Punjab](24751.md) | [Lahore](125315.md) | 127,688,922 |
| [Sindh](28972.md) | [Karachi](17123.md) | 55,696,147 |
| [Khyber Pakhtunkhwa](21950.md) | [Peshawar](66230.md) | 40,856,097 |
| [Gilgit-Baltistan](18807336.md) | [Gilgit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilgit) | 1,492,924 |

| [Islamabad Capital Territory](15294.md) | [Islamabad](51153.md) | 2,363,863 |

Pakistan, a [federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_republic) [parliamentary republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic), consists of [four provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Provinces_\(Pakistan\)): Punjab, Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Sindh, and Balochistan, along with three [territories](446017.md): [Islamabad Capital Territory](15294.md), [Gilgit-Baltistan](18807336.md), and [Azad Kashmir](2745.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdibelli_et_al.2022-288] The Government of Pakistan governs the [western parts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Kashmir) of the [Kashmir Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir_Region), organised into separate political entities, Azad Kashmir and Gilgit-Baltistan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJan2015-289] In 2009, the [constitutional assignment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Pakistan) (the *Gilgit–Baltistan Empowerment and Self-Governance Order*) granted Gilgit-Baltistan [semi-provincial status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Pakistan), providing it with self-government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELansfordMuller2012-290]&#x20;

The [local government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Pakistan) system consists of [districts](639402.md), [tehsils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehsil), and [union councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Councils_of_Pakistan), with an elected body at each tier.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBermanSabharwal2017-291]&#x20;
